As far as I can tell, it's just the people at my job. I mean, I don't see why anyone outside the county office would even care about my inspection reports or the budget stuff. So, no, it seems to strictly be an internal thing with the people I work with.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work would be doing this to you if you hadn't received the county safety award?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean... probably not. I was just doing my job before all that, and nobody had a problem with my reports then. \n\nIt was only after my name got put on that award that everything changed and they started digging into my budget numbers. So, no, I don't think they'd be coming after me if I hadn't won it. It just put a target on my back, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work are trying to harm you in any way beyond just your professional reputation?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, nobody's threatened me physically or anything like that. It's really just about the job. They just want to ruin my credibility at work and make me look like I don't know what I'm doing. So, no, I don't feel like I'm in any physical danger or anything outside of work. It's just my career they're after.\n\nDoctor: Why do you think the people at work are trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: I mean, I think it all comes back to that safety award. Like I said, everything was fine before that. But once I got recognized, it's like... I don't know, maybe they're jealous or they think I don't deserve it. Or maybe they think if they can find a mistake in my inspection reports or my budget numbers, they can prove the county made a mistake giving it to me. It's like they're digging for something to make me look crooked. My supervisor just brushes it off, you know, tells me it's just a normal review process. But I know what a normal review looks like, and this isn't it. It's obvious to me that it's a coordinated effort. You don't need a signed confession to see what's happening right in front of you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone outside of your workplace is involved in this effort to discredit you?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. As far as I can tell, it's just the people at my job. I mean, I don't see why anyone outside the county office would even care about my inspection reports or the budget stuff. So, no, it seems to strictly be an internal thing with the people I work with.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work would be doing this to you if you hadn't received the county safety award?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean... probably not. I was just doing my job before all that, and nobody had a problem with my reports then. \n\nIt was only after my name got put on that award that everything changed and they started digging into my budget numbers. So, no, I don't think they'd be coming after me if I hadn't won it. It just put a target on my back, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work are trying to harm you in any way beyond just your professional reputation?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, nobody's threatened me physically or anything like that. It's really just about the job. They just want to ruin my credibility at work and make me look like I don't know what I'm doing. So, no, I don't feel like I'm in any physical danger or anything outside of work. It's just my career they're after.\n\nDoctor: Why do you think the people at work are trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: I mean, I think it all comes back to that safety award. Like I said, everything was fine before that. But once I got recognized, it's like... I don't know, maybe they're jealous or they think I don't deserve it. Or maybe they think if they can find a mistake in my inspection reports or my budget numbers, they can prove the county made a mistake giving it to me. I just think they want my position, or at least they want to make sure I don't move up any higher than them.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that your supervisor and coworkers might be genuinely trying to review your reports and budget numbers for legitimate reasons, rather than trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: I mean, I've tried to look at it that way. My supervisor actually told me exactly that—he says it's just a normal review. But... it just doesn't add up to me. \n\nEver since that county safety award, it's like certain people are deliberately twisting my inspection reports. They keep asking these really pointed budget questions, almost like they're trying to make me look crooked. So even with my supervisor saying it's just routine, I can just tell there's a coordinated effort going on to discredit me. It's definitely not just a normal review.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were being discredited or targeted at work before you received the county safety award?\n\nPatient: No, not before that. Like I said, everything was completely fine before the award. It's really just been going on for about a year now, right after I got recognized for that county safety initiative. Before that, I never had any issues with anyone.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were being targeted or discredited outside of your job?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. It's really just at work. Outside of my job, everything is perfectly normal. My friends, my family, people around town... no one treats me any differently. It's strictly the people at the department, and only since that award.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I just think they want my position, or at least they want to make sure I don't move up any higher than them.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that your supervisor and coworkers might be genuinely trying to review your reports and budget numbers for legitimate reasons, rather than trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: I mean, I've tried to look at it that way. My supervisor actually told me exactly that—he says it's just a normal review. But... it just doesn't add up to me. \n\nEver since that county safety award, it's like certain people are deliberately twisting my inspection reports. They keep asking these really pointed budget questions, almost like they're trying to make me look crooked. So even with my supervisor saying it's just routine, I can just tell there's a coordinated effort going on to discredit me. It's definitely not just a normal review.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were being discredited or targeted at work before you received the county safety award?\n\nPatient: No, not before that. Like I said, everything was completely fine before the award. It's really just been going on for about a year now, right after I got recognized for that county safety initiative. Before that, I never had any issues with anyone.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were being targeted or discredited outside of your job?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. It's really just at work. Outside of my job, everything is perfectly normal. My friends, my family, people around town... no one treats me any differently. It's strictly the people at the department, and only since that award.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were being targeted or discredited in any previous job or situation before your current one?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like this has ever happened before. Every other job I've had, things were fine. I did my work, got along with my bosses and coworkers, and went home. This whole situation is completely new, and it's only happening here, at this job, right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that people were out to get you or harm you in some way before this situation at work started?\n\nPatient: No, never. I mean, I don't have a history of thinking people are after me or anything like that. Before this whole thing started at the department, I never worried about anyone trying to harm me or ruin my life. It's really just... it's just this specific situation right now. Everything was completely normal before that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any concrete evidence, like documents or recordings, that supports your belief that people at work are trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: I don't have, like, secret recordings or an email where they admit it, if that's what you mean. It's like they're digging for something to make me look crooked. My supervisor just brushes it off, you know, tells me it's just a normal review process. But I know what a normal review looks like, and this isn't it. It's obvious to me that it's a coordinated effort. You don't need a signed confession to see what's happening right in front of you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone outside of your workplace is involved in this effort to discredit you?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. As far as I can tell, it's just the people at my job. I mean, I don't see why anyone outside the county office would even care about my inspection reports or the budget stuff. So, no, it seems to strictly be an internal thing with the people I work with.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work would be doing this to you if you hadn't received the county safety award?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean... probably not. I was just doing my job before all that, and nobody had a problem with my reports then. \n\nIt was only after my name got put on that award that everything changed and they started digging into my budget numbers. So, no, I don't think they'd be coming after me if I hadn't won it. It just put a target on my back, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work are trying to harm you in any way beyond just your professional reputation?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, nobody's threatened me physically or anything like that. It's really just about the job. They just want to ruin my credibility at work and make me look like I don't know what I'm doing. So, no, I don't feel like I'm in any physical danger or anything outside of work. It's just my career they're after.\n\nDoctor: Why do you think the people at work are trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: I mean, I think it all comes back to that safety award. Like I said, everything was fine before that. But once I got recognized, it's like... I don't know, maybe they're jealous or they think I don't deserve it. Or maybe they think if they can find a mistake in my inspection reports or my budget numbers, they can prove the county made a mistake giving it to me. I just think they want my position, or at least they want to make sure I don't move up any higher than them.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that your supervisor and coworkers might be genuinely trying to review your reports and budget numbers for legitimate reasons, rather than trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: I mean, I've tried to look at it that way. My supervisor actually told me exactly that—he says it's just a normal review. But... it just doesn't add up to me. \n\nEver since that county safety award, it's like certain people are deliberately twisting my inspection reports. They keep asking these really pointed budget questions, almost like they're trying to make me look crooked. So even with my supervisor saying it's just routine, I can just tell there's a coordinated effort going on to discredit me. It's definitely not just a normal review.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were being discredited or targeted at work before you received the county safety award?\n\nPatient: No, not before that. Like I said, everything was completely fine before the award. It's really just been going on for about a year now, right after I got recognized for that county safety initiative. Before that, I never had any issues with anyone.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were being targeted or discredited outside of your job?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. It's really just at work. Outside of my job, everything is perfectly normal. My friends, my family, people around town... no one treats me any differently. It's strictly the people at the department, and only since that award.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were being targeted or discredited in any previous job or situation before your current one?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like this has ever happened before. Every other job I've had, things were fine. I did my work, got along with my bosses and coworkers, and went home. This whole situation is completely new, and it's only happening here, at this job, right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that people were out to get you or harm you in some way before this situation at work started?\n\nPatient: No, never. I mean, I don't have a history of thinking people are after me or anything like that. Before this whole thing started at the department, I never worried about anyone trying to harm me or ruin my life. It's really just... it's just this specific situation right now. Everything was completely normal before that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any concrete evidence, like documents or recordings, that supports your belief that people at work are trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: I don't have, like, secret recordings or an email where they admit it, if that's what you mean. But the proof is in what they're doing. Ever since I got that county safety award, certain people at work have been twisting my inspection reports around. They keep asking these really pointed budget questions, like they're trying to make me look crooked. My supervisor keeps saying it's just a normal review, but I can tell. I know how things normally run, and there is definitely a coordinated effort to discredit me. I don't need a secret document to see what's happening right in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that you might be misinterpreting the actions of your coworkers and supervisor?\n\nPatient: No, I... I really don't think I am. I mean, like I was just saying, the timing is what makes it so obvious. It all started right after I got that county safety award. That's when they started twisting my inspection reports and asking those pointed budget questions to make me look crooked. \n\nMy supervisor tells me the exact same thing you're asking—that it's just a normal review and I'm reading too much into it. But I know my job. I know how a standard review works, and this isn't it. I can just tell there is a coordinated effort to discredit me. I'm not misinterpreting what they're doing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. You don't need a signed confession to see what's happening right in front of you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone outside of your workplace is involved in this effort to discredit you?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. It just put a target on my back, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work are trying to harm you in any way beyond just your professional reputation?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, nobody's threatened me physically or anything like that. It's really just about the job. They just want to ruin my credibility at work and make me look like I don't know what I'm doing. I work as a transportation inspector, and... well, ever since I got this county safety award, certain people at work have been coming after me. They’ve been twisting my inspection reports around and asking these really pointed budget questions. It's like they’re trying to make me look crooked or something. My supervisor keeps telling me it’s just a normal review, but I can tell. There’s definitely a coordinated effort to discredit me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any proof that people at work are actually trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: Well, the proof is in what they're doing. Like I was just saying, it all started right after I got that county safety award. You don't just suddenly start getting all these pointed budget questions for no reason. \n\nThey take my daily inspection reports and they... they twist the details around. It's like they're digging for something to make me look crooked. My supervisor just brushes it off, you know, tells me it's just a normal review process. But I know what a normal review looks like, and this isn't it. It's obvious to me that it's a coordinated effort. You don't need a signed confession to see what's happening right in front of you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone outside of your workplace is involved in this effort to discredit you?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. As far as I can tell, it's just the people at my job. I mean, I don't see why anyone outside the county office would even care about my inspection reports or the budget stuff. So, no, it seems to strictly be an internal thing with the people I work with.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work would be doing this to you if you hadn't received the county safety award?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean... probably not. I was just doing my job before all that, and nobody had a problem with my reports then. \n\nIt was only after my name got put on that award that everything changed and they started digging into my budget numbers. So, no, I don't think they'd be coming after me if I hadn't won it. It just put a target on my back, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people at work are trying to harm you in any way beyond just your professional reputation?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, nobody's threatened me physically or anything like that. It's really just about the job. They just want to ruin my credibility at work and make me look like I don't know what I'm doing. So, no, I don't feel like I'm in any physical danger or anything outside of work. It's just my career they're after.\n\nDoctor: Why do you think the people at work are trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: I mean, I think it all comes back to that safety award. Like I said, everything was fine before that. But once I got recognized, it's like... I don't know, maybe they're jealous or they think I don't deserve it. Or maybe they think if they can find a mistake in my inspection reports or my budget numbers, they can prove the county made a mistake giving it to me. I just think they want my position, or at least they want to make sure I don't move up any higher than them.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that your supervisor and coworkers might be genuinely trying to review your reports and budget numbers for legitimate reasons, rather than trying to discredit you?\n\nPatient: I mean, I've tried to look at it that way. My supervisor actually told me exactly that—he says it's just a normal review. But... it just doesn't add up to me. \n\nEver since that county safety award, it's like certain people are deliberately twisting my inspection reports. They keep asking these really pointed budget questions, almost like they're trying to make me look crooked. It's definitely not just a normal review.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were being discredited or targeted at work before you received the county safety award?\n\nPatient: No, not before that. Like I said, everything was completely fine before the award. It's really just been going on for about a year now, right after I got recognized for that county safety initiative. Before that, I never had any issues with anyone.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were being targeted or discredited outside of your job?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. It's really just at work. No, I don't think I would, because they already try to do that. My supervisor keeps telling me that it’s just a normal review process. But I know what I'm seeing.
